4   CRITICAL ACCOUNTING ESTIMATES AND JUDGEMENTS

    Estimates and judgements are continually evaluated. They are based on historical experience and other
    factors, including expectations of future events that may have a financial impact on the entity and that are
    believed to be reasonable under the circumstances.


    Management of the Group makes estimates and assumptions concerning the future. The resulting accounting
    estimates will, by definition, seldom equal the related actual results. The estimates and assumptions that
    have significant risk of causing a material adjustment to the carrying amounts of assets and liabilities within
    the next financial year are addressed below:


    (a)   Recoverability of non-financial assets

          The Group tests annually whether goodwill has suffered any impairment. The recoverable amount of
          a CGU is determined based on value-in-use calculations which require the use of assumptions. The
          calculations use cash flow projections based on financial budgets approved by management covering
          a five-year period. Cash flows beyond the five-year period are extrapolated using the estimated growth
          rates. These growth rates are consistent with forecasts included in industry reports specific to the
          industry in which each CGU operates. Details of impairment charge, key assumptions which are made
          by the management and third-party valuer and impact of possible changes in key assumptions are
          disclosed in Note 16. Based on assessment, the management and directors of the Company are of
          the view that there is no impairment of goodwill as at December 31, 2025.


          Other non-financial assets are reviewed for impairment whenever events or changes in circumstances
          indicate that the carrying amount may not be recoverable. The recoverable amounts have been
          determined based on value-in-use calculations or fair value less costs to sell. These calculations
          require the use of judgments and estimates.


          Judgement is required to determine key assumptions adopted in the valuation models for impairment
          review purpose. Changing the assumptions selected by management in assessing impairment could
          materially affect the result of the impairment test and as a result affect the Group’s financial condition
          and results of operations. If there is a significant adverse change in the key assumptions applied, it may
          be necessary to take additional impairment charge to the consolidated statement of comprehensive
          income.
168 MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025


    Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements




    4     CRITICAL ACCOUNTING ESTIMATES AND JUDGEMENTS (continued)
          (b)   Measurement of the expected credit losses

                The measurement of the expected credit losses for financial assets measured at amortised cost is
                an area that requires the use of complex models and significant assumptions about future economic
                conditions and credit behaviour. Explanation of the inputs, assumptions and estimation techniques
                used in measuring expected credit loss is further detailed in Note 3.1(b).


                A number of judgements are also required in applying the accounting requirements for measuring
                expected credit loss, such as:


                •      Determining criteria for significant increase in credit risk;


                •      Choosing appropriate models and assumptions for the measurement of expected credit loss;
                       and


                •      Establishing the number and relative weightings for forward-looking scenarios and the associated
                       expected credit loss.


          (c)   Recognition of share-based compensation expenses

                The fair value of options is determined by the Binomial model or Monte Carlo model at the grant
                date, and is expected to be expensed over the respective vesting period. Significant estimate on
                assumptions, including underlying equity value, risk-free interest rate, expected volatility, dividend
                yield, and terms, are made by the directors and the third-party valuer.


          (d)   Current and deferred income taxes

                The Group is subject to income taxes in the PRC and other jurisdictions. Judgment is required in
                determining the provision for income taxes in each of these jurisdictions. There are transactions and
                calculations during the ordinary course of business for which the ultimate tax determination is uncertain.
                Where the final tax outcome of these matters is different from the amounts that were initially recorded,
                such differences will impact the income tax and deferred income tax provisions in the period in which
                such determination is made.
                                                                       MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025 169


                                             Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements




4   CRITICAL ACCOUNTING ESTIMATES AND JUDGEMENTS (continued)
    (d)   Current and deferred income taxes (continued)

          The Group considers whether it is probable that the relevant authority will accept each tax treatment,
          or group of tax treatments, that it used or plans to use in its income tax filing, by assuming taxation
          authority will examine those amounts and will have full knowledge of all relevant information. When the
          Group concludes that it is probable that a particular tax treatment is accepted, the Group determines
          taxable profit/(tax loss), tax bases, unused tax losses, unused tax credits or tax rates consistently
          with the tax treatment included in its income tax filings. If the Group concludes that it is not probable
          that a particular tax treatment is accepted, the Group uses the most likely amount or the expected
          value of the tax treatment when determining taxable profit/(tax loss), tax bases, unused tax losses,
          unused tax credits and tax rates. The Group assesses its judgements and estimates if facts and
          circumstances change.


          Deferred income tax assets relating to certain temporary differences and tax losses are recognised
          when management considers it is probable that future taxable profits will be available against which
          the temporary differences or tax losses can be utilized. When the expectation is different from the
          original estimate, such differences will impact the recognition of deferred income tax assets and
          taxation charges in the period in which such estimate is changed.


    (e)   Fair value measurement of FVPL and FVOCI

          The fair value determination of FVPL and FVOCI for which there is no observable market price requires
          the use of valuation techniques. The inputs to the valuation models are taken from observable markets
          where possible, but where this is not feasible, a degree of judgment is required in establishing fair
          values. Details of the key assumptions and inputs used are disclosed in Note 3.3.


    (f)   Scope of consolidation

          Consolidation is required only if control exists. The Group controls an investee when it has all the
          following: (i) power over the investee; (ii) exposure, or rights, to variable returns from its involvement with
          the investee; and (iii) the ability to use its power over the investee to affect the amount of the Group’s
          returns. Power results from rights that can be straightforward through voting rights or complicated in
          contractual arrangements. Variable returns are returns that are not fixed and have the potential to vary
          as a result of the performance of an investee. These three factors cannot be considered in isolation by
          the Group in its assessment of control over an investee. Where the factors of control are not apparent,
          significant judgement is applied in the assessment, which is based on an overall analysis of all of the
          relevant facts and circumstances.


          The Group is required to reassess whether it controls the investee if facts and circumstances indicate
          a change to one or more of the three factors of control.
170 MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025

    (a)   Pensions – defined contribution plans

          Majority of the Group’s contributions to pension plans are related to the local employees in the PRC. All
          local employees of the subsidiaries in the PRC participate in employee social security plans established
          in the PRC, which cover pension, medical and other welfare benefits. The plans are organised and
          administered by the governmental authorities. The Group also participates in the Mandatory Provident
          Fund Scheme in Hong Kong, under which the Group and relevant employees are required to contribute
          to the scheme monthly, and the Group has no further payment obligations once the contributions
          have been paid. The Group’s contributions to the Mandatory Provident Fund Scheme are expensed
          as incurred. Except for the contributions made to these social security plans, the Group has no other
          material commitments owing to the employees. According to the relevant regulations, the portion of
          premium and welfare benefit contributions that should be borne by the companies within the Group
          as required by the above social security plans are principally determined based on percentages of
          the basic salaries of employees, subject to certain ceilings imposed. These contributions are paid
          to the respective labour and social welfare authorities and are expensed as incurred. No forfeited
          contributions were used to reduce the current year’s level of contributions (2024: Nil) and no forfeited
          contribution was available at December 31, 2025 (2024: Nil) to reduce future years’ contributions.

    11    INCOME TAX EXPENSES (continued)
          (c)   PRC corporate income tax

                CIT provision was made on the estimated assessable profits of entities within the Group incorporated
                in the PRC and was calculated in accordance with the relevant regulations of the PRC after considering
                the available tax benefits from refunds and allowances. The general PRC CIT rate is 25% for the years
                ended December 31, 2025 and 2024.


                One subsidiary of the Group in Chinese Mainland was approved as High and New Technology
                Enterprise and was subject to a preferential tax rate of 15% for the year ended 31 December 2025
                and 2024.


          (d)   BVI income tax

                No provision for income tax in BVI has been made as the Group has no income assessable to income
                tax in BVI for the years ended December 31, 2025 and 2024.


          (e)   Withholding tax

                According to applicable tax regulations prevailing in the PRC, dividends distributed by a company
                established in Chinese Mainland to a foreign investor with respect to profit derived after January 1,
                2008 are generally subject to a 10% withholding tax.

           Diluted earnings per share are calculated by adjusting the weighted average number of shares
           outstanding to assume conversion of all dilutive potential shares. For the years ended December 31,
           2025 and 2024, the Company had dilutive potential ordinary shares of share options and RSUs granted
           to employees (Note 30). The number of shares that would have been issued assuming the exercise
           of the share options less the number of shares that would have been issued at assumed exercise
           price (determined as any amount that the employees must pay upon exercise and the balance of
           any amounts calculated under IFRS Accounting Standards that have not yet been charged to income
           statement) are incremental shares issued for nil consideration which causes dilution to earnings per
           share. The number of shares that would have been issued also assuming the vest of the RSUs less
           the number of shares that would have been issued at assumed consideration (determined as the
           balance of any amounts calculated under IFRS Accounting Standards that have not yet been charged
           to income statement) are incremental shares issued for nil consideration which causes dilution to
           earnings per share.

    13    DIVIDENDS

          A final dividend in respect of the year ended December 31, 2024 of HKD0.32(equivalent to RMB0.29) per
          share was approved by the shareholders of the Company at the annual general meeting on June 25, 2025.
          Shareholders were given the option to elect to receive the final dividend only in cash or only in script shares.
          As a result, 10,734,653 ordinary shares were issued for a total consideration of HKD78,298,559 (equivalently
          approximately RMB71,474,840), and the remaining was paid in cash.


          The Board has recommended the payment of a final dividend of HKD0.22 per share out of the distributable
          reserves for the year ended December 31, 2025, subject to the approval of the shareholders at the 2026
          annual general meeting of the Company or any adjournment thereof. The final dividend proposed after the
          end of the reporting period will be either payable in cash or in form of new fully paid shares of the Company
          in respect of all of such final dividend at shareholders’ option. This proposed dividend is not reflected as
          dividend payable in the consolidated financial statements.

     Goodwill impairment

     The goodwill balance mainly arose from the acquisition of 100% equity interests in Beijing Weige Shidai
     Entertainment Technology Co., Ltd (“Beijing Weige Shidai”) and Shenzhen Ruihai Fangyuan Technology
     Co., Ltd. (“Ruihai Fangyuan”) in 2017, and the acquisition of Hangzhou Soushi Network Co., Ltd (“Hangzhou
     Soushi”) in 2019. Goodwill is attributable to the acquired market share and economies of scale expected
     to be derived from combining with the operations of the Group. The remaining goodwill was related to the
     acquisition of Chengdu Kandao Culture Media Co., Ltd. in 2025, which was immaterial to the Group (Note
     36) and no impairment was made.


     Upon completion of acquisition of 100% equity interest in Beijing Weige Shidai and Ruihai Fangyuan in
     2017, and Hangzhou Soushi in 2019, the Group integrated the business (including the management, assets,
     customers, users and systems) of Beijing Weige Shidai, Ruihai Fangyuan and Hangzhou Soushi with the
     Group’s operations in order to improve the operation efficiency. Goodwill of approximately RMB4,504,884,000
     arising from the acquisition of Beijing Weige Shidai, Ruihai Fangyuan and Hangzhou Soushi was allocated
     to the group of CGUs.


     Impairment review on the goodwill of the Group has been conducted by the management as at December
     31, 2025. For the purpose of impairment review, the recoverable amount of the CGU is determined based on
     value-in-use calculations. These calculations use pre-tax cash flow projections based on financial budgets
     prepared by management covering a five-year period.
186 MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025


    Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements




    16    INTANGIBLE ASSETS (continued)
          Goodwill impairment (continued)

          The key parameters used for value-in-use calculations on the movie ticketing operations include revenue
          growth rates, gross margin, terminal growth rate, and pre-tax discount rate:


                                                                                            As at December 31,

                                                                                                2025                 2024



          Annual revenue growth rate for five-year period                               -6.4%-13.0%           6.1%-20.2%
          Gross margin                                                                 40.0%-47.3%          44.8%-52.2%
          Terminal growth rate                                                                  2.0%                 2.0%
          Pre-tax discount rate                                                              16.69%                16.98%


          i)     Revenue growth rates

                 Revenue is directly related to the value of paid transactions on the Group’ platform and box office of
                 the Group’s business partners. The revenue growth rates are estimated with reference to the industry
                 growth forecast for the market in which the Group operates. When estimating the revenue growth rate
                 of the five-year period, the directors of the Company referred to the industry outlook of the film market.


          ii)    Gross Margin

                 The budgeted gross margin of the five years between 40.0% and 47.3% (2024: 44.8% and 52.2%)
                 was determined by the management based on past performance, the current market conditions and
                 its expectation for market development. For items of cost of revenue that relating to gross online
                 entertainment ticketing services of the Group, the Group referred to current fee rate to project the
                 ticket system cost and internet infrastructure cost.


          iii)   Terminal growth rate

                 Cash flows beyond the five-year period are extrapolated using the estimated terminal growth rates
                 of 2.0% (2024: 2.0%).
                                                                      MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025 187


                                            Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements




16   INTANGIBLE ASSETS (continued)
     Goodwill impairment (continued)

     iv)   Discount rate

           The discount rate used is pre-tax and reflects market assessments of the time value and the specific
           risks relating to the industry. With the assistance of a valuation performed by a third-party independent
           valuer, the directors used cash flow projections based on the latest financial budget covering a five-year
           period and business plan approved by management which had been updated to reflect the changes
           in market conditions during the period, The directors of the Company considered the uncertainty in
           PRC entertainment industry due to the enhancement of government regulations and a pre-tax discount
           rate of 16.69% (2024: 16.98%) was applied.


           Based on the result of the goodwill impairment testing, the estimated recoverable amount of the business
           far exceeded its carrying amount as at December 31, 2025 by approximately RMB2,351,593,000
           (December 31, 2024: RMB4,230,083,000).


           The management of the Group has not identified that a reasonable possible change in any of the key
           assumptions that could cause the carrying amount to exceed the recoverable amount.


           The Group performs the sensitivity analysis based on the assumptions that revenue growth rate,
           gross margin, terminal growth rate or the discount rate have been changed. Had the estimated key
           assumptions during the forecast period been changed as below, the headroom would be decreased
           to as below:

    23    PREPAYMENTS, FILM RIGHTS, DEPOSITS AND OTHER RECEIVABLES (continued)
          (a)   The Group, as a producer, produces films with co-producers or by the Group, which is considered as one of the
                principal activities of the Group. This amount represents the film rights production costs invested by the Group.

          (b)   The Group, as a distributor, offers distribution and promotion services, which is considered as one of the principal
                activities of the Group. This amount represents the advance payments to film producers for the distribution and
                promotion contracts which will be deducted from revenue, when movie distribution and promotion services are
                rendered.

          (c)   The impairment provision mainly represents impairment of prepayments for distribution and promotion contracts and
                film rights production costs. For the year ended December 31, 2025, due to factors including but not limited to the
                replacement of major actors of certain projects, the risk that certain films may not be released in the foreseeable
                future, declining expectations for the produced films’ box office, and the deterioration of financial and operational
                position of the producers or co-producers, together with the probability of default, the Group identified the expected
                recoverable amounts of certain film rights production costs could not cover the respective carrying amounts and
                made impairment provision of approximately RMB17,532,000. Additionally, the Group wrote off approximately
                RMB66,130,000 in impairment charges, mainly due to the previously impaired films released during the year
                ended December 31, 2025, and reversed RMB14,500,000 since the Group entered into withdrawal agreements
                with business partner.

          (d)   The amounts mainly represent the distribution and promotion expenses prepaid to the suppliers which would be
                recognised as costs or expenses when the services are provided to the Group.

          (e)   In line with the general industry practice and after prudently considering factors including creditworthiness and
                cooperation relationships with relevant business partners to control potential risk, the Group prepaid deposits or
                advance payments to some cinemas and large-scale concerts organizers operating within Chinese Mainland and
                then deducted or recovered such prepayment during the settlement with such business partners at a later stage.

          (f)   As at December 31, 2025, except for a loan to third party with carrying amount of approximately RMB30,000,000
                repayable on or after May 19, 2027, the remaining loans are repayable within 1 year. Except for interest-free and
                unsecured loans amounting to approximately RMB13,660,000, the remaining loans are interest-bearing at fixed
                rates ranging from 3% to 10% per annum.

          (g)   The amounts mainly represent the investments with fixed returns or refunds in certain films, TV series projects and
                entertainment events.

          ESOP Plan of the Company

          In order to provide incentives and rewards to directors, senior management and employees of the Group
          and other eligible individuals and entities, the Company adopted the ESOP Plan on July 23, 2018. The ESOP
          Plan include Pre-IPO Share Option Scheme, Post-IPO Share Option Scheme and the Post-IPO RSU Scheme.


          The total number of shares issued or issuable pursuant to the ESOP Plan shall not be more than 117,033,705
          shares of the Company, representing approximately 10.1% of the total issued share capital of the Company
          as at December 31, 2025, out of which, the maximum number of shares that may be issued upon exercise
          of all options granted and to be granted under the Pre-IPO Share Option Scheme shall be no more than
          42,544,600 shares, and the total number of shares which may be issued upon exercise of options that may
          be granted under the Post-IPO Share Option Scheme and the RSU Scheme shall not exceed 55,221,880
          shares in aggregate.

30   SHARE INCENTIVE PLAN (continued)
     ESOP Plan of the Company (continued)

     (b)   Post-IPO Share Option Scheme (continued)
           Fair value of options
           The Group has used the binomial lattice model to determine the fair value of the options at the grant date
           during the year ended December 31, 2025. The weighted average fair value of share options granted
           by Maoyan Entertainment during the year ended December 31, 2025 was RMB3.09 per share option.
           Significant judgement on parameters, such as risk-free rate, dividend yield and expected volatility, are
           required to be made by the directors in applying the valuation model, which are summarized as below.


                                                                                                        Year ended
                                                                                                     December 31,
                                                                                                               2025



           Risk-free rate                                                                                      3.1%
           Dividend yield                                                                                      2.0%
           Expected volatility                                                                                55.0%



           The expected volatility, measured as the standard deviation of expected share price returns, is
           determined based on the average daily trading price volatility of the shares of the comparable
           companies.


           Expected retention rate
           The Group has to estimate the expected yearly percentage of grantees that will stay within the Group
           at the end of the vesting periods of the share options (the “Expected Retention Rate”) in order to
           determine the amount of share-based compensation expenses charged to the consolidated statement
           of comprehensive income. As at December 31, 2025, such retention rate was assessed to be close
           to 99.1% (December 31, 2024: 99.3%).
210 MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025


    Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements




    30    SHARE INCENTIVE PLAN (continued)
          ESOP Plan of the Company (continued)

          (c)   Post-IPO RSU Scheme

                On May 6, 2025, the Board of the Company resolved to grant a total of 400,000 restricted share
                units under RSU Scheme to employees of the Group. A total of 200,000, 100,000 and 100,000 of the
                restricted shares will vest on May 6, 2027, May 6, 2028 and May 6, 2029 respectively, subject to the
                accomplishment of certain service conditions. The fair value of the restricted shares amounted to

36   SUBSIDIARIES (continued)
     Business combinations

     During the year ended 31 December 2025, the Group completed the acquisition of 50% equity interest of a
     media content studio at a cash consideration of RMB15,000,000, which was accounted for as a subsidiary
     of the Group upon the completion of the transaction.


     The fair value of total identifiable net assets (including identifiable intangible assets) was approximately
     RMB18,719,000.


     Goodwill of approximately RMB5,641,000 was recognised as a result of the transaction. It was mainly
     attributable to the operating synergies and economies of scale expected to be derived from combining the
     operations. None of the goodwill was expected to be deductible for income tax purpose.


     The Group’s revenue and results for the year ended 31 December 2025 would not be materially different
     should the acquisition had occurred on January 1, 2025.


     The related transaction costs of the transaction recognised in the Group’s consolidated income statement
     were not material.

38   FROZEN EQUITY INTERESTS OF TIANJIN MAOYAN WEYING CULTURAL MEDIA CO.,
     LTD.

     As disclosed in Note 2.2.7, Tianjin Maoyan Weying is a PRC subsidiary controlled by the Group through
     contractual arrangements. Beijing Shiji Weying Culture Development Co., Ltd. (“Beijing Shiji Weying”) is a
     registered shareholder of Tianjin Maoyan Weying and holds approximately 26.9% equity interests (the “Equity
     interests”) of Tianjin Maoyan Weying. As at December 31, 2025, the Equity Interests were frozen due to the
     following cases:


     (a)   Frozen case 480

           Beijing Weying Shidai Technology Co., Ltd. (“Beijing Weying Shidai”), an affiliated company of Beijing
           Shiji Weying, which transferred the Equity Interests to Beijing Shiji Weying in 2018 for preparation
           the listing of the Company on the Main Board of The Stock Exchange of Hong Kong Limited (the
           “Transfer”). Prior to the Transfer, Beijing Weying Shidai originally entered into contractual agreements
           with the WFOE, Tianjin Maoyan Weying and its registered shareholders in July 2018. Subsequent to
           the Transfer, Beijing Shiji Weying signed the contractual agreements in August 2018 as part of the
           contractual arrangements as disclosed in Note 2.2.7.


           Since Beijing Weying Shidai was involved in certain debt disputes involving approximately USD6,058,000
           (equivalent to approximately RMB40.2 million, the “Debt”) with a creditor who initiated lawsuits against
           Beijing Weying Shidai as defendant, a court in the PRC issued a civil paper (Document 2021 Jing
           04 Zhi 480) (“Civil Paper 480”), pursuant to which the Equity Interests were frozen for the purpose of
           cancelling the Transfer and transferring the Equity Interests back to Beijing Weying Shidai.


           In May 2022, Beijing Shiji Weying and Beijing Weying Shidai have entered into settlement agreement
           with the debtor and agreed to repay the amounts by instalments before March 30, 2024. As at the
           approval date of this financial statements, Beijing Shiji Weying and Beijing Weying Shidai has repaid
           RMB20.1 million out of the total RMB40.2 million but failed to repay the remaining instalments of
           RMB20.1 million as agreed in the settlement agreement.


     (b)   Frozen case 1258

           Beijing Weying Shidai was involved in another debt disputes involving a principal of approximately
           RMB75 million and relevant interest calculated at an interest rate of 12% per annum with a shareholder
           of Beijing Weying Shidai who initiated an arbitration against Beijing Weying Shidai as respondent, a
           court in the PRC issues a civil paper (Document 2022 Jing 01 Zhi 1258) (“Civil Paper 1258”), pursuant
           to which the Equity Interests were frozen due to above mentioned debt.
228 MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025


    Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements




    38    FROZEN EQUITY INTERESTS OF TIANJIN MAOYAN WEYING CULTURAL MEDIA CO.,
          LTD. (continued)
          (b)   Frozen case 1258 (continued)

                Beijing Shiji Weying and Beijing Weying Shidai are still in the process of negotiating with the creditors
                on the settlement of the debt in dispute. As at December 31, 2025 and the date of approval of the
                financial statement, the Equity Interests are remained frozen and cannot be changed due to above
                cases.


                In response to the above cases, Beijing Shiji Weying and Beijing Weying Shidai co-issued a letter of
                confirmation (the “Confirmation”) to Tianjin Maoyan Weying and WFOE, pursuant to which they agreed
                to comply and fulfil all the terms and conditions, responsibilities and obligations under the contractual
                agreements including but not limited to fully cooperating when WFOE exercises its irrevocable and
                exclusive right to purchase the Equity Interests, or transfer the Equity Interests to WFOE’s assignee
                at WFOE’s request.


                The directors of the Company, based on the advice of its PRC legal advisors, considered that the
                Contractual Arrangements disclosed in the Note 2.2.7 and the Confirmation are in compliance with
                relevant PRC laws and regulations and are legally binding and enforceable. The Company considers
                the risk of the Equity Interests been disposed due to above two cases is remote. There is no significant
                change for these contractual arrangements and the consolidation of Tianjin Maoyan Weying.

    Environmental, Social and Governance Report


    I.    ABOUT THE REPORT

          This report is aimed at disclosing the performance and results in respect of environmental, social and
          governance (“ESG”) of Maoyan Entertainment (the “Company or “We”) to all the stakeholders in 2025. It is
          prepared in accordance with the Appendix C2 Environmental, Social and Governance Reporting Code (the
          ESG Reporting Code) of the Listing Rules of the Main Board of the Stock Exchange of Hong Kong Limited and
          in compliance with the requirements of principles of “materiality”, “quantitative”, “balance” and “consistency”
          set out in the Code.


          (1)    Materiality: The Company has conducted a materiality assessment of ESG issues by inviting internal
                 and external stakeholders to participate in identifying, assessing and prioritizing the materiality of such
                 ESG-related issues, and disclosed the process and results of the materiality assessment in this report.
                 The Board has completed reviewing the results of the materiality assessment of the ESG issues.


          (2)    Quantitative: This report disclosed relevant quantitative environmental and social data of the Company,
                 and explained the standards and methods used for their statistics and calculations.


          (3)    Balance: This report objectively disclosed positive and negative information to ensure the balance of
                 its contents.


          (4)    Consistency: The data disclosed in this report adopted the same statistical methodology and reporting
                 scope as previous years to ensure the comparability of its contents.


          This report covers the period from January 1, 2025 to December 31, 2025, with some data being retrospect to
          previous years. The information disclosed in this report is mainly derived from the statistical data and related
          documents of the Company. The Company confirms that this report does not contain any false records and
          misleading statements, and the Company is responsible for the authenticity, accuracy and completeness
          of its content.


          Unless otherwise stated, based on materiality considerations, the scope of disclosure in this report includes
          the Company and its subsidiaries in China, and covers the same scope as that in the annual report.


          This report is issued together with the annual report of the Company. For the content of corporate governance,
          readers are reminded to read this report in conjunction with the section headed Corporate Governance
          Report in the annual report.
                                                                     MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025 231


                                               Environmental, Social and Governance Report




II.   BOARD STATEMENT

      The Company has always regarded ESG governance as a materiality issue in the course of corporate
      management and operations, and has continuously explored ESG-specific governance practices, with a view
      to elevating the ESG management capabilities, and integrating the concept of sustainable development into
      the Company’s overall strategy. As the highest decision-making body of ESG matters that is responsible for
      formulating and implementing the Company’s overall ESG strategy, the Board of Directors of the Company
      takes responsibility for identifying, assessing, monitoring, and managing the ESG risks, reviewing the
      assessment results of the ESG materiality issues, as well as promoting the implementation of ESG strategies,
      policies, goals and key tasks. In addition, the Board of Directors regularly examines the progress made in
      its ESG goals. The ESG taskforce is composed of the audit department, legal affairs department, human
      resources, administration, and other departments of the Company, and is responsible for assisting the
      Board in promoting the execution of the specific ESG work, as well as making regular reports to the Board
      of Directors on ESG risk assessment, key issues of concern, progress made in the goals, and other ESG-
      related work. Such practice will ensure that the Company will carry out all ESG-related tasks in a steady and
      orderly manner. Each business department, in conjunction with the actual work, identifies relevant ESG issues
      within the respective business areas, collaborates in the implementation of ESG management strategies and
      working plans, and provides feedback to the ESG taskforce on the progress of related work.
232 MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025


    Environmental, Social and Governance Report




    III.   ESG MANAGEMENT PHILOSOPHY AND STRATEGY

           The Company places great emphasis on environmental, social and governance matters. A three-tier ESG
           governance structure has been established, comprising the Board, the ESG taskforce, and various business
           departments. The Board serves as the highest decision-making body, responsible for overseeing and
           reviewing the Company’s overall ESG strategies and material issues. The ESG taskforce, composed of the
           Company’s senior management and heads of functional departments, is collectively responsible for the
           planning, coordination, implementation of measures, compliance management, and risk control of ESG-
           related work. Business departments, as the execution level, implement specific ESG measures and action
           plans based on their respective operational circumstances. Each level operates with clear role definitions
           and collaborative synergy, thereby providing robust support for the Company’s sustainable development. The
           Company continues to strengthen its ESG management capacity. During the year, one ESG training session
           was held for the Directors, with a duration of 0.5 hours. The training covered topics including evolving trends
           in ESG issues, reinforcement and implementation of ESG management measures, and definition of planning
           objectives at various stages. In light of regulatory and market developments, this training has effectively
           enhanced the ESG accountability and competency of the management personnel for the coming years.


                                            Table: ESG Governance Structure


            Board of Directors                 •      To approve the overall ESG strategies, goals, and core tasks;
            (Decision-making Level)            •      To coordinate the identification, assessment, and supervision of
                                                      ESG-related risks;
                                               •      To review ESG progress reports regularly to facilitate the

            ESG Taskforce                      •      Responsible for the specific planning, coordination, and
            (Management Level)                        execution of ESG-related work;

                                                      matters, and achievement of goals on a regular basis.

            (Execution Level)                         action plans within their respective business areas based on

                                               •      To promote the integration of ESG requirements into daily

                                         Environmental, Social and Governance Report




As a leading integrated service platform of “Technology + Pan-Entertainment” in China, we always adhere to
the concept of sustainable development, and integrate ESG governance into the Company’s overall strategic
planning and daily operations. We continuously optimize the ESG governance mechanism, regularly examine
the effectiveness of ESG management efforts, identify risks and opportunities in operations, and actively
explore ways to enhance ESG performance, to ensure the full realization of sustainable development goals.
At the same time, we continue to invest in technological innovation, content distribution, user experience, and
other areas, promoting a healthy industry ecosystem through responsible corporate practices, and creating
sustainable, comprehensive value for our shareholders, users, employees, and society.


Given our ESG management philosophy, we propose the following ESG management strategies:


•     Environmental protection: to implement the notion of green office practices, by carrying out low-
      carbon, energy-saving, and environmental measures to promote the green business development, and
      actively responding to climate change challenges to promote and strengthen the climate resilience.


•     Talent management: to optimize the talent development and management system, strive to safeguard
      employee health and safety, and grow together with employees.


•     Consumer protection: to protect intellectual property rights, pursue responsible marketing practices,
      create a high-quality and healthy content ecosystem, strengthen data security and privacy protection,
      strengthen communication with customers to protect the consumer interests and rights.


•     Community care: to actively engage in community charity as our joint efforts to build a harmonious
      community ecosystem.

          key SDGs issues of concern, and incorporates the concept of sustainable development into its business

    VI.   GREEN ECOLOGY AND LOW-CARBON OPERATIONS

          As a key player in the cultural and entertainment consumption sector, the Company actively supports the
          national “Dual Carbon” goals by integrating environmental management into its corporate strategy and daily
          operations, thereby systematically advancing the construction of its environmental governance system. The
          Company continuously enhances its environmental management systems, promotes resource conservation
          and energy efficiency, and actively explores pathways to align low carbon operations with business innovation,
          demonstrating its commitment to fulfilling environmental responsibilities while delivering commercial value.


          1.    The Environment and Natural Resources

                The Company strictly complies with the Environmental Protection Law of the People’s Republic of
                China and other laws and regulations, and has formulated and improved its systems, including the
                Environmental Management System in Office Areas, thereby building an environmental management
                framework. Committed to the concept of sustainable development, the Company carefully assesses
                the potential impact of its business activities on the environment and natural resources, and proactively
                implements energy saving and emission reduction initiatives to minimize the negative impact of its
                operations on the environment. As the Company’s core operations, namely entertainment content
                services, online ticketing services, and advertising business, do not involve large scale manufacturing,
                their direct impact on the environment and natural resources is limited. Nevertheless, we remain
                dedicated to deepening our green operational practices and exploring more environmentally friendly
                business models, thereby contributing to the sustainable development of both the industry and the
                society.


                In the performance business segment, the Company actively responded to the national call for
                energy conservation and emission reduction, and systematically advanced the green transformation
                of its business operations. In 2025, we fully promoted electronic ticketing products. This led to a
                cumulative reduction of over 17.86 million paper tickets, significantly lowering paper consumption
                and its associated environmental impact.


                In terms of hardware equipment management, we adhere to green procurement principles and prioritize
                the selection of ticket validator, ticket dispenser, and other equipment that comply with environmental
                and quality management system certifications. The Company’s new generation of equipment has
                achieved improvements in both energy efficiency and material use, significantly reducing energy
                consumption. During the equipment recycling stage, we require partner suppliers to hold green
                recycling qualifications and to adopt disposal methods that meet environmental standards, ensuring
                that environmental responsibility is upheld throughout the equipment’s entire lifecycle.
                                                                MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025 241


                                        Environmental, Social and Governance Report




     To reduce the carbon footprint in operations, we optimized a nationwide warehousing and logistics
     system in 2025, with four regional warehouses located in Langfang, Chengdu, Shanghai, and
     Guangzhou. By optimising warehouse layouts and logistics routes, we effectively reduced equipment
     transportation distances, achieving a cumulative saving of approximately 111,200 kilometers in
     transportation mileage throughout the year. This provided necessary infrastructure for realizing low-
     carbon operations.


     In addition to embedding green concepts into our core businesses and operations, we also extend
     environmental protection into employee care and corporate culture by actively exploring innovative
     ways to reduce resource consumption through the optimisation of our internal welfare distribution model.


      Case: Green Benefits

      To put into practice the concept of a circular economy, we have made “green” optimisations to
      our employee holiday benefits. For packaging materials, the Dragon Boat Festival gift boxes use
      environmentally friendly DuPont paper. This material combines recyclability with durability, significantly
      reducing raw material consumption compared to traditional packaging. In terms of model innovation,
      we introduced digital benefit distribution plans for the Spring Festival and the Mid Autumn Festival,
      replacing physical customised gift boxes with electronic “Maoxiang Cards (貓享卡)”. This eliminates
      the production, transportation, and disposal of packaging materials at the source. Through this
      innovative approach that integrates the “use of eco friendly materials” with the “digitalisation of
      benefit models”, we are advancing new practices for environmentally responsible corporate culture.


2.   Use of Resources and Energy

     The Company strictly complies with the Energy Conservation Law of the People’s Republic of China
     and other relevant regulatory requirements. It has incorporated provisions on energy conservation
     and energy efficiency improvement into the Employee Manual, and advances these efforts through
     institutionalised management, employee awareness-raising, and technological optimisation. The
     Company has put in place an energy management system covering key areas such as electricity
     and water usage, and strengthens company-wide energy conservation awareness through thematic
     campaigns and daily reminders, thereby ensuring the effective implementation of all relevant measures.
242 MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025

                2.1    Electricity Management

                       In terms of electricity management, the Company integrates energy-saving requirements into its
                       internal rules and continuously reduces energy consumption through technological optimisation.
                       The Company’s administrative management policies explicitly require employees to switch
                       off lighting, air conditioning, and equipment power when leaving the office area, promoting
                       the timely shutdown of non-essential power sources. In addition, the Company prioritizes the
                       procurement of high-efficiency equipment, promotes LED energy-saving lighting, and sets air
                       conditioning temperatures appropriately, thereby reducing operational energy consumption
                       and carbon emissions through technological upgrades, while actively fulfilling its environmental
                       responsibilities.


                2.2    Water Management

                       In terms of water management, we adopt a combination of hardware measures and behavioural
                       guidance to continuously improve our water management system. We actively promote
                       water-saving devices, maintain a regular inspection and maintenance mechanism, and
                       encourage employees to promptly report leaks, use environmentally friendly detergents, and
                       avoid wasteful practices. To enhance water utilization efficiency, we have implemented a reject
                       water recovery project for drinking water, using the treated reject water for cleaning purposes.
                       This initiative saves over 50 tonnes of water annually.
                                                                           MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025 243


                                                 Environmental, Social and Governance Report




           2.3     Packaging Material Management

                   The Company’s core businesses, namely online ticketing and entertainment content services,
                   operate under an asset light internet platform model. As its operating activities do not involve
                   physical production or manufacturing processes, there is no associated consumption of
                   packaging materials.

          3.    Emissions Management

                The Company’s daily operations do not involve exhaust gas emissions or discharges into water and
                land, and therefore have a limited direct environmental impact. We are always committed to minimizing
                the environmental footprint in our operations, and continue to fulfill environmental responsibilities in
                areas such as waste reduction and sorting, compliant disposal, and resource conservation by improving
                systems, strengthening management, and promoting active engagement.


                3.1    Waste Reduction and Sorting

                       To effectively reduce resource consumption and promote recycling, we implement waste
                       reduction and sorting management measures. We promote digitalised office operations and
                       paperless approval processes, set up paper recycling bins, encourage double-sided printing
                       and paper reuse, and strive to reduce paper usage and the associated waste generation.


                       In terms of sorting management, we actively respond to national waste sorting policies, place
                       categorized waste bins in office areas, and continuously provide environmental awareness
                       education and behavioral guidance to employees through the posting of instructional posters.
                       This ensures that our sorting measures are fully implemented and continuously improved.


                3.2    Compliant Disposal of Waste

          4.     Responses to Climate Change

                 To address climate change and enhance climate resilience and sustainability, the Company continuously
                 enhances its climate governance framework, and systematically identifies, assesses, and manages
                 climate-related risks and opportunities.


                 4.1    Governance

                        To effectively address climate change, the Company has established a three-tier governance
                        framework consisting of the Board, the ESG taskforce, and the business departments, ensuring
                        the top-down integration of climate-related risk and opportunity identification, management,
                        and action. The Board, as the governance level, is responsible for approving climate strategies,
                        overseeing key risks, and regularly reviewing progress. The ESG taskforce, at the management
                        level, plans and coordinates climate actions, organizes risk assessments, and handles information
                        disclosure. At the operational level, each business department implements relevant measures
                        based on its specific operations, embedding climate management into daily processes and
                        building a systematic response capability.




    4
          Hazardous wastes involved in the operation of the Company primarily include waste toner cartridges and waste toners of

    5
          Non-hazardous wastes involved in the operation of the Company primarily include office waste and disposed office
          supplies.

                    4.2        Strategy

                               The Company integrates climate-related impacts and opportunities into its corporate strategy,
                               actively monitoring physical risks such as extreme weather and global warming, as well as
                               transition risks including government regulation, market competition, and brand and reputation.
                               We implement climate change response measures, embed preventive actions into daily
                               operations and business decisions, and enhance climate resilience. At the same time, we
                               focus on the potential impacts and market opportunities arising from market changes and
                               brand building, actively seize green consumption trends, and continuously explore ways to
                               promote environmentally friendly products and service models in the cultural and entertainment
                               consumption sector. We strive to reduce our operational carbon footprint, driving sustainable
                               development through business innovation, and actively contributing to global efforts to address
                               climate change.

                         4.3       Risk Management

                                   The Company integrates climate change responses into its overall corporate risk management
                                   system. We have established a closed-loop management process of “risk assessment – risk
                                   response – monitoring and review”, seamlessly embedding the identification, response, and
                                   monitoring of climate risks into daily operations. We are committed to continuously improving
                                   our adaptability and response capabilities to climate change, ensuring the long-term resilience
                                   of the Company’s strategy and business.
                                                            MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025 251


                                  Environmental, Social and Governance Report




                          Table: Risk Management Process


       Risk          •      Risk identification: Identify potential uncertainties brought by climate change
       Assessment           across business processes to develop a preliminary risk checklist.
                     •      Risk analysis: Analyze the risks in the checklist, assess their potential impact
                            and likelihood, and use this assessment to determine risk levels and priorities.
                     •      Risk evaluation: Synthesize the risk analysis results to form the Company’s overall
                            climate change risk assessment report, clearly identifying key risk areas.

       Risk          •      Strategy formulation and execution: Based on the risk assessment report, develop
       Response             an enterprise level overall response strategy. Accordingly, each business and
                            functional department develops and executes specific response plans.
                     •      Process reporting: Each department regularly reports on the implementation
                            progress and status of risk response measures.

       Monitoring    •      Regularly review and evaluate the overall execution of climate change risk
       and Review           management.
                     •      Summarize effectiveness and shortcomings, and propose recommendations to
                            continuously optimize and enhance the management process.


4.4   Metrics and Targets

      The Company treats greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions as a key indicator for measuring climate
      change and continues to advance GHG emission reduction efforts to lower its operational
      carbon footprint. Given that the Company plans to relocate its office in 2026, the size, layout,
      and facility configurations of the current office area will be adjusted accordingly, and the energy
      mix may undergo phased changes. To ensure the scientific basis and practicality of its carbon
      targets, the Company intends to systematically establish carbon reduction goals aligned with
      its development needs once the relocation has been completed and operations have stabilized.

    VII. TALENT DEVELOPMENT AND SOCIAL RESPONSIBILITY

          The Company believes that every employee has unlimited potential and creativity. Upholding the cultural
          values of “Integrity, Excellence, Collaboration, and Happiness”, the Company is committed to enhancing its
          employee management and talent development systems while creating a fair, inclusive, and vibrant workplace.


          1.     Talent Management

                 The Company strictly complies with the Labor Law of the People’s Republic of China, the Labor Contract
                 Law of the People’s Republic of China, the Social Insurance Law of the People’s Republic of China, and
                 other relevant laws and regulations. It adheres to the principles of equality and diversity throughout the
                 entire employment process, maintains a scientific and rational compensation and benefits system, and
                 has established a human resources development mechanism centered on learning and development,
                 continuously improving employee satisfaction and engagement.


                 1.1     Talent Attraction and Diversity

                         Through diverse recruitment channels and an open talent philosophy, the Company safeguards
                         employee rights and actively fosters a sustainable workplace environment.




    7
          As the Company is not engaged in physical production or manufacturing, and does not own any conventional fuel powered

          its GHG emissions consist primarily of indirect emissions associated with purchased electricity under Scope 2. GHG
          emissions are presented in carbon dioxide equivalents and calculated based on the Notice on the Release of CO2 Emission
          Factors for Electricity in 2023 issued by the Ministry of Ecology and Environment of the People’s Republic of China and

Employee Employment
Centered on the management philosophy that talent is the primary driver, the Company aligns
with its long-term strategic planning by continuously recruiting external talent and developing
internal talent, while systematically advancing employee hiring and talent development efforts.
In response to the Company’s business development needs, regular organizational workforce
reviews and forecasts are conducted to identify talent requirements and formulate recruitment
plans. The Company continues to optimize and diversify its recruitment channels, leveraging
diverse channels such as recruitment platforms, campus recruitment, internal referrals, and
headhunters to attract talent. The Company places particular emphasis on its “Campus
Recruitment Program”, which offers targeted internship opportunities to current students.
Through one-on-one mentoring and cross-departmental collaboration, the program helps convert
interns into a key source of future talent for the Company. In addition, the Company actively
promotes and supports an internal referral mechanism, under which current employees may
recommend external candidates for recruitment and selection, and those who make successful
referrals receive incentives. During the Reporting Period, the Company hired 184 employees,
providing stable talent support for its business development and organizational capability
building.

Employee Rights and Diversity
The Company is committed to building a diverse talent team and places great importance on
diversity and talent protection. The Company strictly prohibits any form of illegal employment.
During the recruitment process, it verifies candidates’ identity documents, graduation certificates,
and other relevant materials to confirm their age and identity, effectively preventing the risk of
employing minors. When assigning tasks, the Company fully considers and respects employees’
skills, qualifications, and work preferences, maintaining a scientific and standardized division
of labor and work processes. The Company has a zero-tolerance policy toward any form of
forced labor. Any violations will be dealt with strictly in accordance with laws and regulations.
In 2025, the Company recorded no cases of minor employment or forced labor. The Company
also strictly complies with all legal and regulatory provisions regarding working hours and leave
entitlements. Through the Employee Manual and policies on human resources management, the
Company clearly sets forth the fundamental principles of respect, fair treatment, and inclusion,
ensuring that employees do not suffer unfair treatment based on age, race, color, religion,
ethnicity, gender, region, nationality, sexual orientation, or disability. The Company opposes
all forms of discrimination, harassment, offensive conduct, or disparagement. Employees can
provide feedback through whistleblowing channels for violations; if substantiated, the Company
will take serious action.
256 MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025


    Environmental, Social and Governance Report




                       Guided by these principles, the Company continuously advocates and creates a diverse and
                       friendly workplace atmosphere and offers a range of benefits to address the professional needs
                       of its diverse workforce, continuously energizing corporate development. Currently, female
                       employees represent over 54% of the Company’s total workforce, over 51% of management
                       positions, 52% of junior management roles, 63% of revenue-generating roles, and 24% of
                       STEM-related positions.


                1.2    Employee Development

                       The Company has established a comprehensive compensation and benefits and performance
                       evaluation mechanism, systematically promotes employee development and training centered
                       on employee career development and capability enhancement, and builds employee career
                       development paths to achieve two-way empowerment of the enterprise and talent.


                       Compensation, Benefits, and Performance
                       The Company determines salaries based on a comprehensive consideration of position value,
                       performance outcomes, and individual capabilities, while striking a balance between internal
                       pay equity and external market competitiveness. The Company offers an employee share
                       ownership plan and a special reward mechanism for core employees, thereby enhancing the
                       range and effectiveness of employee incentives.


                       The Company operates a dynamic performance evaluation mechanism, conducting regular
                       performance evaluation for all employees through a closed-loop performance management
                       system that includes goal setting, self-assessment, supervisor evaluation, and personal
                       development plan creation, with varying evaluation cycles tailored to the needs of different
                       roles. Evaluation results are directly applied to salary adjustments, bonus payouts, and talent
                       development. The Company values transparency in the performance process, maintaining
                       two-way communication throughout the performance management cycle, and supports employee
                       development through result discussions and improvement coaching. In addition, the Company
                       has put a performance appeal mechanism in place to safeguard the fairness and transparency
                       of the performance management process.


                       The Company continuously enhances career development pathways and capability enhancement
                       support, maintaining a diverse portfolio of talent development initiatives. It operates a
                       dual-track promotion system featuring parallel management and professional career tracks,
                       with assessments and feedback provided through a review process conducted by a review
                       committee composed of cross-departmental experts.
                                                  MAOYAN ENTERTAINMENT / Annual Report 2025 257


                           Environmental, Social and Governance Report

learning. Beyond self-directed learning, the Company offers a range of learning opportunities
and resources tailored to business needs, job requirements, and individual characteristics
to support employee development. These include, but are not limited to: classroom training,
online learning resources, on-the-job coaching, job-specific skills training, and the assignment
of challenging projects.

                1.3    Employee Care and Support

                       The Company systematically enhances employee satisfaction and cohesion through initiatives
                       such as welfare protection, cultural activities, and communication mechanisms.


                       Employee Benefits
                       The Company has built a multi-level protection system that includes both statutory and
                       supplementary benefits. It pays social insurance and housing fund in full according to law,
                       and provides supplementary commercial medical insurance, annual physical examination, and
                       accident insurance for all employees. In terms of leave policies, the Company strictly adheres
                       to national regulations and fully implements maternity leave, paternity leave, parental leave,
                       and paid annual leave, thereby further strengthening institutional support for employees’ family
                       responsibilities.


                       In addition, the Company implements a flexible work policy to help alleviate commuting stress
                       and improve work efficiency by allowing flexible start and end times. Based on the work
                       characteristics of different positions, the Company also introduces alternative work hour

                        In 2025, the film The Stage (戲台) mirrored the contemporary survival story of the endangered
                        opera troupe “Wuqing Troupe”(五慶班), profoundly exploring the dilemma of heritage and
                        innovation of traditional culture amidst the tide of the times. Beyond cinematic storytelling, the
                        film served as a platform for broader cultural engagement. The Company collaborated with
                        professional opera academies to organize special screenings, focusing on “how traditional
                        arts can engage in dialogue with contemporary society”. These events encouraged younger
                        audiences to move beyond superficial curiosity and develop a deeper understanding of the
                        intrinsic value and cultural significance of traditional opera. At the same time, to enrich the
                        cultural and recreational life of audiences aged 60 and above and improve cinema attendance

                        event. The initiative engaged over 3,000 cinemas nationwide, with 262,306 senior admissions
                        recorded. In addition, the social benefits of cultural products are extended into real-world
                        contexts, helping traditional Chinese arts to revitalize and thrive in the contemporary era.

Case: The Film Panda Plan: The Magical Tribe (熊貓計劃之部落奇遇記) Assisting Wa Ethnic
Culture Heritage

In 2025, the film Panda Plan: The Magical Tribe , produced by the Company, was shot in
Wengding Village, Cangyuan Wa Autonomous County, Yunnan Province, widely known as
“China’s last primitive tribe”. Leveraging the village’s well-preserved traditional Wa ethnic
architecture and folk culture, the film integrates distinctive Wa cultural elements into the
storyline, profoundly presenting the unique charm of ethnic culture. During the filming process,
the film crew strictly adhered to cultural preservation principles to avoid any adverse impact
on the traditional village. At the same time, through the medium of film and television, the
film helped promote Wa folk customs to the public, providing an audio-visual vehicle for the
protection and transmission of ethnic culture. This demonstrates the Company’s responsible
practice in supporting the dissemination of ethnic culture through cultural products.
